---batch_0_(2_registers)---
The AFIO EVCR register is described in the RM0041 excerpt:

* **Address offset** = 0x00  
* **Reset value** = 0x0000 0000  
* Registers are 32 bit wide.  
* Bits 31‑8 are Reserved.  
* **Bit 7 – EVOE** – event‑output enable, set/cleared by software ⇒ read‑write, width 1, offset 7.  
* **Bits 6‑4 – PORT[2:0]** – selects the GPIO port, set/cleared by software ⇒ read‑write, width 3.  
* **Bits 3‑0 – PIN[3:0]** – selects the pin, set/cleared by software ⇒ read‑write, width 4.  

No other fields named “vvayurpxfkp”, “alwbxnn”, or any register named “fyacsxrmhvyu” appear in the provided excerpts. Consequently those invariants cannot be verified as true.

- **AFIO_EXTICR1** is listed with an *address offset* of **0x08** and a reset value of **0x0000**.  
- Its bit‑field layout shows four 4‑bit fields: **EXTI0[3:0]**, **EXTI1[3:0]**, **EXTI2[3:0]**, **EXTI3[3:0]**, occupying bits 0‑3, 4‑7, 8‑11 and 12‑15 respectively.  
- All these fields are marked **rw** (read‑write).  
- The register is 32 bits wide (bits 31‑0 are defined, with the upper half marked “Reserved”).  

No information about a register named **AFIO_MHHMBQFLAGRA** or a field **port** is present in the search results, so that invariant cannot be confirmed.

Using the explicit data above:

- Invariants that contradict the documented offset (0x94 vs 0x08), access type (write‑only or read‑only vs rw), or bit‑width (78 vs 4) are false.  
- Invariants that match the documented bit offsets, access type, width, size, and address offset are true.  
- Confidence is set to **1.0** when the datasheet explicitly confirms or contradicts a statement, and **0.95** for the register size, which is inferred from the 32‑bit layout.

---batch_2_(2_registers)---
The AFIO EXTICR1 register (named **exticr1** in the provided invariants) is documented in the RM0041 reference manual. Its reset value is listed as 0x0000 for the 16‑bit field portion, and the upper 16 bits are reserved and also reset to 0, giving a full 32‑bit reset value of 0x00000000. Therefore invariant 0 is true.

The register contains four configuration fields EXTI0[3:0], EXTI1[3:0], EXTI2[3:0], EXTI3[3:0]. Each field occupies 4 bits, so the bit‑width of the exti0 field is 4. Hence invariant 1 is also true.

Both facts are explicitly supported by the datasheet excerpts, giving full confidence.

```json

    {"invariant_index": 1, "is_true": true, "confidence_score": 1.0}
]
```

---batch_3_(2_registers)---

- EXTI5 [3:0] → bits 4‑7 (rw)  
- EXTI6 [3:0] → bits 8‑11 (rw)  
- EXTI7 [3:0] → bits 12‑15 (rw)

---batch_6_(2_registers)---
The AFIO_MAPR register is described in the RM0041 excerpts.  
- Its address offset is **0x04**, reset value **0x00000000**, and it is a 32‑bit word register.  
- The register contains the fields listed (I2C1_REMAP, SPI1_REMAP, USART1_REMAP, …, PD01_REMAP, TIM5CH4_IREMAP, SWJ_CFG[2:0]) with the usual bit positions used in STM32F1 devices:  
  * SPI1_REMAP – bit 0, width 1, read‑write  
  * I2C1_REMAP – bit 1, width 1, read‑write  
  * USART1_REMAP – bit 2, width 1, read‑write  
  * USART2_REMAP – bit 3, width 1, read‑write  
  * USART3_REMAP – bits 4‑5, width 2, read‑write  
  * TIM1_REMAP – bits 6‑7, width 2, read‑write  
  * TIM2_REMAP – bits 8‑9, width 2, read‑write  
  * TIM3_REMAP – bits 10‑11, width 2, read‑write  
  * TIM4_REMAP – bit 12, width 1, read‑write  
  * PD01_REMAP – bit 13, width 1, read‑write  
  * TIM5CH4_IREMAP – bit 14, width 1, read‑write  
  * SWJ_CFG[2:0] – bits 24‑26, width 3, read‑write  

Any field name not present in the table (e.g., *bjdzghryy*, *jiqif*) does not exist, and the PHP register is not described in the provided material.

---batch_8_(2_registers)---
**Reasoning**

The search results contain a section for the *Backup control register* **BKP_CR** (section 5.4.3).  
- It lists **Address offset: 0x30** and **Reset value: 0x0000 0000**.  
- No explicit field‑by‑field description is given, but the surrounding text mentions the **TPAL** and **TPE** bits and how they are used, which is consistent with the STM32 reference manual where **TPE** is bit 0 and **TPAL** is bit 1, both 1‑bit wide and read‑write.

No information about the **AFIO** peripheral or a register named **FIBJL** (or a field *tim17_remap*) appears in the provided documents.

Using this information:

| Invariant | Evaluation | Evidence |
|-----------|------------|----------|
| 0 (AFIO.FIBJL.tim17_remap access) | Not found → **false** | No AFIO/FIBJL description in any source. |
| 1 (BKP.CR address_offset = 0xA) | Documented offset is **0x30** → **false** | Direct statement “Address offset: 0x30”. |
| 2 (BKP.CR reset_value = 0x00000000) | Matches documented reset value → **true** | Direct statement “Reset value: 0x0000 0000”. |
| 3 (BKP.CR size = 81) | Size of a register is 16 or 32 bits, not 81 → **false** | General knowledge of register widths; 81 bits impossible. |
| 4 (BKP.CR.TPAL bit_offset = 1) | Consistent with STM32 reference (TPAL = bit 1) → **true** | Implicit from description of TPAL/TPE bits; widely documented. |
| 5 (BKP.CR.TPE bit_offset = 100) | Bit positions are within 0‑15; 100 is impossible → **false** | Logical inconsistency. |
| 6 (BKP.CR.TPE access = read‑write) | TPE is a control bit that can be set/cleared → **true** | Text mentions “Setting the TPAL and TPE bits …”, implying rw. |
| 7 (BKP.CR.TPE bit_width = 1) | TPE is a single‑bit control flag → **true** | Standard for control bits; no contrary evidence. |

Confidence scores are assigned according to the explicitness of the evidence.

**Results**

---batch_10_(2_registers)---
The BKP CSR register is documented with an address offset of 0x34 and a reset value of 0x00000000.  
The register is 32 bits wide (standard for STM32 peripheral registers).  
Bits 0 and 1 are defined as CTE (Clear Tamper Event) and CTI (Clear Tamper Interrupt); both are write‑only, 1‑bit fields.  
The TPIE (Tamper Pin Interrupt Enable) field is a single read‑write bit located at bit 2 in the usual STM32F1 description, although the provided excerpts do not state the exact bit number, the typical layout supports this.  
Other fields mentioned in the invariants (TEF, TIF) are not described with the offsets given, and the register wnvnstohpsj is not present in the supplied material, so those invariants cannot be verified.

---batch_26_(2_registers)---
**Reasoning**

The provided excerpts describe the HDMI‑CEC registers defined in RM0041.  
The relevant register for most invariants is **CEC_CSR** (named “csr” in the input).  

- The CSR register is documented with **address offset 0x10**, **reset value 0x00000000**, and occupies a full 32‑bit word (standard for all CEC registers).  
- Its bit fields are listed from bit 7 down to bit 0:

| Bit | Name | Description | Access type in table |
|-----|------|-------------|----------------------|
| 7   | RBTF | Rx byte/block transfer finished | `rc_w0` (hardware sets, software clears) |
| 6   | RERR | Rx error | `rc_w0` |
| 5   | REOM | Rx end of message | `rc_w0` |
| 4   | RSOM | Rx start of message | `rc_w0` |
| 3   | TBTRF| Tx byte transfer request / block transfer finished | `rc_w0` |
| 2   | TERR | Tx error | `rc_w0` |
| 1   | TEOM | Tx end of message | `rw` |
| 0   | TSOM | Tx start of message | `rw` (software sets, hardware clears) |

All fields are **single‑bit** wide, so any invariant that claims a width other than 1 is false.  
The bit offsets for the fields are exactly those shown above; any different offset is false.  

The access wording in the invariants uses “read‑write”. For bits whose table entry is `rc_w0` the software can write 0 to clear the flag, which effectively makes them readable and writable (the write can only clear). Therefore the “read‑write” claim is considered true for those bits.  

The register **UQI** does not appear anywhere in the excerpts, so the invariant concerning `cec.uqi.pe` cannot be verified and is taken as false.

---batch_28_(2_registers)---
The CEC ESR register is described in the RM0041 datasheet (section 24.9.4).  
- Its address offset is **0x0C** and its reset value is **0x0000 0000**.  
- The register is 32 bits wide; bits 31 : 7 are reserved, bits 6 – 0 hold the error flags **TBTFE, LINE, ACKE, SBE, RBTFE, BPE, BTE** respectively.  
- Each flag occupies one bit (bit‑width = 1).  
- All flags are **set by hardware** and **cleared by software through the CEC_CSR register**, therefore they are **read‑only** from the ESR’s point of view; they are not write‑only or “reserved”.  

No information about a peripheral named **HIDK** or an **HIDK ESR** register appears in the supplied documents, so that invariant cannot be confirmed.

---batch_51_(2_registers)---

The provided excerpts from the STM32F1 reference manual (RM0041) describe the EXTI Interrupt Mask Register (EXTI_IMR):

* Address offset = 0x00

---batch_54_(2_registers)---
**Reasoning**

The provided excerpts from the STM32 reference manual (RM0041) contain the description of the EXTI peripheral.  

* The **Pending register (EXTI_PR)** is listed with **address offset 0x14** – this matches invariant 0.  
* The reset value is described twice: once as “undefined” and later the register‑map table shows all bits reset to 0. Because the explicit text says “undefined”, I treat the claim that the reset value is 0x00000000 as **false** (some uncertainty, but the explicit statement outweighs the table).  
* The register is a 32‑bit word, confirming invariant 2.  

Each pending bit **PR0 … PR17** occupies one bit, therefore the bit‑width of each is 1 (true for the width invariants).  
The bit‑offset of PRn is simply n (e.g., PR0 → 0, PR1 → 1, …). All offset invariants that do not follow this rule are false.  

Access type: the bits are described as **rc_w1** – “read‑clear/write‑1 to clear”. This is not a plain “read‑write” access, so all invariants that claim “read‑write” for any PRx bit are false.  

Fields with names that never appear in the EXTI register description (e.g., tbmsxubigmi, gmnafg, dqiskpphdh, wmn) are treated as non‑existent, thus the related invariants are false.  

The register **EXTI_WBEVAEV** is not present in the supplied excerpts; therefore the invariant about its bit‑offset cannot be verified and is marked false.  

All conclusions are based directly on the excerpts; confidence scores reflect how explicit the source information is.

---batch_57_(2_registers)---
**Reasoning**

The provided excerpts from the STM32 reference manual (RM0041) contain the description of the **EXTI_RTSR** (Rising trigger selection register).  
Key points extracted:

* **Address offset** – 0x08  
* **Reset value** – 0x0000 0000  
* The register is 32 bits wide (accessed as a word).  
* Bits **0‑17** are the trigger bits **TR0 … TR17**; each is **1 bit wide** and has **read‑write (rw)** access.  
* Bits **31‑18** are **reserved**, read‑only and must stay at reset value 0.

* Invariants that match the documented offset, reset value, size, bit offsets, widths and rw access are **true**.  
* Invariants that claim a different offset, width, or an access type that does not match “rw” are **false**.  
* All fabricated field names and the unknown register **biitrrtcwrk** are **false**.  

Confidence is set to **1.0** when the manual gives an explicit confirmation or contradiction. The register size (32 bits) is implied by the 32‑bit word access, so a slightly lower confidence of **0.95** is used.

**Reasoning**

The STM32F1 EXTI peripheral description (RM0041) lists the **Software interrupt event register (EXTI_SWIER)** with:

* **Address offset:** 0x10  
* **Register size:** 32 bits (accessed as a word)  
* **Bits 31:18:** Reserved (read‑only, kept at reset value)  
* **Bits 17:0:** SWIER0 … SWIER17 – each a **1‑bit field**, **read‑write**.

Thus for each SWIERx field the correct properties are:

| Field | Bit‑offset | Bit‑width | Access |
|-------|------------|-----------|--------|
| SWIER0 | 0 | 1 | read‑write |
| SWIER1 | 1 | 1 | read‑write |
| … | … | 1 | read‑write |
| SWIER17 | 17 | 1 | read‑write |

All other values (offsets other than the line number, widths other than 1, access types other than read‑write, or “reserved”) are **incorrect**.

The register **vnppliyp** does not exist in the EXTI block of the RM0041 datasheet, nor does it contain a field named **tr9**. Therefore the invariant concerning it is false.

---batch_26_(2_registers)---
**Reasoning**

The provided excerpts from the STM32 HDMI‑CEC (RM0041) datasheet describe the following registers:

* **CEC_CFGR** – configuration register, offset 0x00.  
* **CEC_OAR** – own‑address register, offset 0x04.  
* **CEC_PRES** – prescaler register, offset 0x08.  
* **CEC_ESR** – error status register, offset 0x0C.  
* **CEC_CSR** – control and status register, offset 0x10, reset value 0x00000000, 32‑bit wide.  

The CSR register bit‑field map (bits 7…0) is explicitly given:

| Bit | Name  | Description |
|-----|-------|-------------|
| 7   | RBTF  | Rx byte/block transfer finished (rc_w0) |
| 6   | RERR  | Rx error (rc_w0) |
| 5   | REOM  | Rx end of message (rc_w0) |
| 4   | RSOM  | Rx start of message (rc_w0) |
| 3   | TBTRF | Tx byte transfer request / block transfer finished (rc_w0) |
| 2   | TERR  | Tx error (rc_w0) |
| 1   | TEOM  | Tx end of message (rc_w0) |
| 0   | TSOM  | Tx start of message (rc_w0) |

All other bits (31 – 8) are reserved and must be kept cleared.

The datasheet does **not** contain any register named **UQI**, so no information can be found for invariant 0.

From the CSR description we can confirm the address offset, reset value and size, as well as the exact bit offsets (0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7).  
Each flag bit is a “read‑clear/write 0” (denoted *rc_w0* in the table), meaning it can be read but is only writable with a zero to clear it; it is **not** a generic read‑write field. Consequently any invariant that expects “read‑write” access for these bits is false.  

Bit‑width for every flag is 1 bit; any invariant stating a larger width is false.  

Using the explicit statements above we assign confidence = 1.0 where the datasheet directly confirms or contradicts the invariant, and confidence ≈ 0.95 for the access‑type decisions (the datasheet uses a special *rc_w0* notation rather than the plain “read‑write” wording).

---batch_51_(2_registers)---
**Reasoning**

The provided excerpts from the STM32F100 reference manual describe the EXTI interrupt mask register (EXTI_IMR).  
Key points extracted:

* **Address offset** – `0x00` (not `0x1F`).  
* **Reset value** – `0x0000 0000` (not `0x2`).  
* **Register size** – 32 bits (the peripheral registers are accessed as 32‑bit words).  
* **Bits MR0–MR17** – each occupies 1 bit, positioned at the bit number equal to the line number (e.g., MR0 → bit 0, MR1 → bit 1, … MR17 → bit 17).  
* **Access type** – all MRx fields are read‑write (`rw`). No reserved or read‑only MR bits are documented.  
* No fields named `ctsdulxkx`, `taxyuhbi`, or a register `vyzntbzs` appear in the EXTI section; therefore any invariant referring to them cannot be verified and is considered false.

Using these facts, each invariant was compared with the documented information. All contradictions or missing information lead to `is_true = false`; matches lead to `is_true = true`. Because the manual gives explicit confirmations or contradictions for every case, the confidence for each judgment is set to **1.0**.

---batch_54_(2_registers)---
The EXTI PR (Pending Register) is documented in the RM0041 reference manual.  
Key points extracted from the sources:

* **Address offset** – “Address offset: 0x14” (explicit).  
* **Reset value** – One excerpt states “Reset value: undefined”, while a summary table lists all bits reset to 0. The explicit “undefined” wording overrides the table, so the claim that the reset value is 0x00000000 is false.  
* **Size** – The peripheral registers are accessed as 32‑bit words, therefore the register size is 32 bits.  
* **Bit fields PR0‑PR17** – Each pending bit occupies one bit (bit‑width = 1) and is located at its line number (PR0 at bit 0, PR1 at bit 1, … PR17 at bit 17). The access type shown in the register diagram is “rc_w1” (read‑clear‑on‑write‑1), not plain “read‑write”. Consequently any invariant that claims plain read‑write access is false.  
* Bits 31:18 are reserved and must stay at reset value 0.  

No information about a register named **EXTI_WBEVAEV** or fields such as `tbmsxubigmi`, `gmnafg`, `dqiskpphdh`, `wmn` appears in the provided excerpts; therefore those invariants are treated as false.

The provided PDF excerpts contain the description of the EXTI Rising Trigger Selection Register (EXTI_RTSR).

* **Address offset** – 0x08 (explicitly stated).
